anddn,h​(fn,h,fn,h∗)2≳εMn​(fn,h∗,h)−Mn​(fn,h,h)d_{n,h}\left(f_{n,h},f_{n,h}^{*}\right)^{2}\gtrsim_{\varepsilon}M_{n}\left(f_{n,h}^{*},h\right)-M_{n}\left(f_{n,h},h\right)(40)

for everyh∈ℋm,εh\in\mathcal{H}_{m,\varepsilon}.
Then, for everynn, everyε>0\varepsilon>0, and every positiveδ≥τn−1​Cε,0​δ¯n\delta\geq\tau_{n}^{-1}C_{\varepsilon,0}\underline{\delta}_{n}for some constantCε,0>0C_{\varepsilon,0}>0depending onε\varepsilon,suph∈ℋm,εsupf∈ℱn:δ/2<dn,h​(f,fn,h)≤δMn​(f,h)−Mn​(fn,h,h)≲ε−τn2​δ2.\sup_{h\in\mathcal{H}_{m,\varepsilon}}\sup_{f\in\mathcal{F}_{n}:\delta/2<d_{n,h}\left(f,f_{n,h}\right)\leq\delta}M_{n}(f,h)-M_{n}\left(f_{n,h},h\right)\lesssim_{\varepsilon}-\tau_{n}^{2}\delta^{2}.(41)

## Lemma 14(Inequality for Rademacher Complexity).

Letξ1,…,ξn\xi_{1},\ldots,\xi_{n}be i.i.d. Rademacher random variables, i.e.,P​(ξi=1)=P​(ξi=−1)=1/2P(\xi_{i}=1)=P(\xi_{i}=-1)=1/2for everyi=1,…,ni=1,\ldots,n, and suppose that they are independent of the data.
Let𝒰\mathcal{U}be a bounded subset ofℝn\mathbb{R}^{n}and for everyi=1,…,ni=1,\dots,n, letϕi:ℝ→ℝ\phi_{i}:\mathbb{R}\rightarrow\mathbb{R}satisfyϕi​(0)=0\phi_{i}(0)=0and be Lipschitz continuous with constantLϕL_{\phi}, i.e.,|ϕi​(u)−ϕi​(v)|≤Lϕ​|u−v||\phi_{i}(u)-\phi_{i}(v)|\leq L_{\phi}|u-v|for everyu,v∈ℝu,v\in\mathbb{R}.
Then, foru=(u1,…,un)′∈𝒰u=(u_{1},\ldots,u_{n})^{\prime}\in\mathcal{U}, we have𝔼​[supu∈𝒰|∑i=1nξi​ϕi​(ui)|]≤2​Lϕ⋅𝔼​[supu∈𝒰|∑i=1nξi​ui|].\mathbb{E}\left[\sup_{u\in\mathcal{U}}\left|\sum_{i=1}^{n}\xi_{i}\phi_{i}(u_{i})\right|\right]\leq 2L_{\phi}\cdot\mathbb{E}\left[\sup_{u\in\mathcal{U}}\left|\sum_{i=1}^{n}\xi_{i}u_{i}\right|\right].

## Lemma 15(Matrix Norm Inequalities,Stewart and Sun,1990, Theorem II.3.9).

Let∥⋅∥\|\cdot\|be a unitarily invariant norm such as the Frobenius norm∥⋅∥F\|\cdot\|_{F}, the spectral norm∥⋅∥sp\|\cdot\|_{\mathrm{sp}}, and the nuclear norm∥⋅∥∗\|\cdot\|_{*}. For any matricesA∈ℝT×KA\in\mathbb{R}^{T\times K},A~∈ℝK×T′\widetilde{A}\in\mathbb{R}^{K\times T^{\prime}}, we have‖A​A~‖≤‖A‖sp⋅‖A~‖and‖A​A~‖≤‖A‖⋅‖A~‖sp.\|A\widetilde{A}\|\leq\|A\|_{\mathrm{sp}}\cdot\|\widetilde{A}\|\quad\text{and}\quad\|A\widetilde{A}\|\leq\|A\|\cdot\|\widetilde{A}\|_{\mathrm{sp}}.

## Lemma 16.

For every matrixA∈ℝT×KA\in\mathbb{R}^{T\times K}with rank at most one, we have‖A‖∗=‖A‖F=‖A‖sp=tr⁡(A′​A)\|A\|_{*}=\|A\|_{F}=\|A\|_{\mathrm{sp}}=\sqrt{\operatorname{tr}(A^{\prime}A)}.
IfA=u​v′A=uv^{\prime}for some vectorsuuandvv, then‖A‖∗=‖A‖F=‖A‖sp=‖u‖2​‖v‖2\|A\|_{*}=\|A\|_{F}=\|A\|_{\mathrm{sp}}=\|u\|_{2}\|v\|_{2}.

## Lemma 17.

For every functiona:𝒱→ℝKa:\mathcal{V}\rightarrow\mathbb{R}^{K}, every matrixA∈ℝT×KA\in\mathbb{R}^{T\times K}, and every probability measurePPon𝒱\mathcal{V}, we have‖A​a‖P,2≤‖A‖sp⋅‖a‖P,2.\|Aa\|_{P,2}\leq\|A\|_{\mathrm{sp}}\cdot\|a\|_{P,2}.

## Lemma 18.

For every matrixA∈ℝT×KA\in\mathbb{R}^{T\times K}withrank⁡(A)≥1\operatorname{rank}(A)\geq 1, the spectral norm of its Moore-Penrose inverse satisfies‖A+‖sp=1/σmin+​(A).\|A^{+}\|_{\mathrm{sp}}=1/\sigma_{\min}^{+}(A).

## Lemma 19(Bound on Effective Degrees of Freedom).

Let𝒩A​(λ)\mathcal{N}_{A}(\lambda)be the effective degrees of freedom defined as𝒩A​(λ)=tr⁡(A​(A+λ2​I)−1)\mathcal{N}_{A}(\lambda)=\operatorname{tr}\left(A(A+\lambda^{2}I)^{-1}\right)for a positive semi-definite matrixA∈ℝK×KA\in\mathbb{R}^{K\times K}andλ>0\lambda>0.
LetA~∈ℝK×K\widetilde{A}\in\mathbb{R}^{K\times K}be another positive semi-definite matrix.
Then,|𝒩A​(λ)−𝒩A~​(λ)|≤‖A−A~‖∗/λ2.\left|\mathcal{N}_{A}(\lambda)-\mathcal{N}_{\widetilde{A}}(\lambda)\right|\leq\|A-\widetilde{A}\|_{*}/\lambda^{2}.

## Lemma 20(Transformation on Effective Degrees of Freedom).

Let𝒩A​(λ)\mathcal{N}_{A}(\lambda)be the effective degrees of freedom defined as inLemma˜19.
LetQ∈ℝK×KQ\in\mathbb{R}^{K\times K}be another matrix.
Then,𝒩Q​A​Q′​(λ)≤max⁡{1,‖Q‖sp2}​𝒩A​(λ).\mathcal{N}_{QAQ^{\prime}}(\lambda)\leq\max\{1,\|Q\|_{\mathrm{sp}}^{2}\}\mathcal{N}_{A}(\lambda).

We bound each term separately.

By˜13inAssumption˜1(ii),(I)=OP​(rta,n).\text{(I)}=O_{P}(r_{\operatorname{ta},n}).

Next, we bound the second term (II). By˜12inAssumption˜1(i),‖gwidecheck∘hwidecheck−gm∘hm‖Pso,2=OPso​(δso,m).\left\|\widecheck{g}\circ\widecheck{h}-g_{m}\circ h_{m}\right\|_{P_{\operatorname{so}},2}=O_{P_{\operatorname{so}}}(\delta_{\operatorname{so},m}).

Hence, for everyε>0\varepsilon>0, there exists a constantMε<∞M_{\varepsilon}<\inftysuch thatlim infm→∞Pso​(ming∈𝒢m⁡‖g∘hwidecheck−gm∘hm‖Pso,2≤Mε​δso,m)≥1−ε.\liminf_{m\rightarrow\infty}P_{\operatorname{so}}\left(\min_{g\in\mathcal{G}_{m}}\left\|g\circ\widecheck{h}-g_{m}\circ h_{m}\right\|_{P_{\operatorname{so}},2}\leq M_{\varepsilon}\delta_{\operatorname{so},m}\right)\geq 1-\varepsilon.

Thus, we havelim infm→∞Pso​(hwidecheck∈ℋso,m​(ρso,m))≥1−ε,\liminf_{m\rightarrow\infty}P_{\operatorname{so}}\left(\widecheck{h}\in\mathcal{H}_{\operatorname{so},m}(\rho_{\operatorname{so},m})\right)\geq 1-\varepsilon,

withρso,m=Mε​δso,m\rho_{\operatorname{so},m}=M_{\varepsilon}\delta_{\operatorname{so},m}.
Since the event{hwidecheck∈ℋso,m​(ρso,m)}\{\widecheck{h}\in\mathcal{H}_{\operatorname{so},m}(\rho_{\operatorname{so},m})\}depends only on the source sample, the above lower bound also holds under the joint lawPP. On this event,Assumption˜2and˜11imply thathwidecheck∈ℋta,m​(ρso,m/νn)\widecheck{h}\in\mathcal{H}_{\operatorname{ta},m}(\rho_{\operatorname{so},m}/\nu_{n}), i.e.,minf∈ℱnsub⁡‖f∘hwidecheck−fn∘hm‖Pta,2≤ρso,m/νn=Mε​δso,m/νn.\min_{f\in\mathcal{F}_{n}^{\operatorname{sub}}}\left\|f\circ\widecheck{h}-f_{n}\circ h_{m}\right\|_{P_{\operatorname{ta}},2}\leq\rho_{\operatorname{so},m}/\nu_{n}=M_{\varepsilon}\delta_{\operatorname{so},m}/\nu_{n}.

Therefore,minf∈ℱnsub⁡‖f∘hwidecheck−fn∘hm‖Pta,2=OPso​(δso,m/νn).\min_{f\in\mathcal{F}_{n}^{\operatorname{sub}}}\left\|f\circ\widecheck{h}-f_{n}\circ h_{m}\right\|_{P_{\operatorname{ta}},2}=O_{P_{\operatorname{so}}}(\delta_{\operatorname{so},m}/\nu_{n}).

Hence, the second term satisfies(II)=OP​(δso,m/νn).\text{(II)}=O_{P}(\delta_{\operatorname{so},m}/\nu_{n}).

ByDefinition˜1,(III)=O​(ϵta,n).\text{(III)}=O(\epsilon_{\operatorname{ta},n}).

Combining the three bounds yields‖f^∘hwidecheck−ata∗‖Pta,2=OP​(rta,n+δso,m/νn+ϵta,n),\left\|\widehat{f}\circ\widecheck{h}-a_{\operatorname{ta}}^{*}\right\|_{P_{\operatorname{ta}},2}=O_{P}\left(r_{\operatorname{ta},n}+\delta_{\operatorname{so},m}/\nu_{n}+\epsilon_{\operatorname{ta},n}\right),

## G.1Proof forLemma˜2

## Proof.

Since the loss function is the mean squared loss,inff∈ℱnsub𝔼​[(f∘h​(Z)−fn∘hm​(Z))2]\displaystyle\inf_{f\in\mathcal{F}_{n}^{\operatorname{sub}}}\mathbb{E}\left[\left(f\circ h(Z)-f_{n}\circ h_{m}(Z)\right)^{2}\right]=inff∈ℱnsub𝔼​[(𝔼​[fn∘hm​(Z)∣h​(Z)]−fn∘hm​(Z)+f∘h​(Z)−𝔼​[fn∘hm​(Z)∣h​(Z)])2]\displaystyle\quad=\inf_{f\in\mathcal{F}_{n}^{\operatorname{sub}}}\mathbb{E}\left[\left(\mathbb{E}\left[f_{n}\circ h_{m}(Z)\mid h(Z)\right]-f_{n}\circ h_{m}(Z)+f\circ h(Z)-\mathbb{E}\left[f_{n}\circ h_{m}(Z)\mid h(Z)\right]\right)^{2}\right]=𝔼​[Var⁡(fn∘hm​(Z)∣h​(Z))]+inff∈ℱnsub𝔼​[(𝔼​[fn∘hm​(Z)∣h​(Z)]−f∘h​(Z))2],\displaystyle\quad=\mathbb{E}\left[\operatorname{Var}\left(f_{n}\circ h_{m}(Z)\mid h(Z)\right)\right]+\inf_{f\in\mathcal{F}_{n}^{\operatorname{sub}}}\mathbb{E}\left[\left(\mathbb{E}\left[f_{n}\circ h_{m}(Z)\mid h(Z)\right]-f\circ h(Z)\right)^{2}\right],

where the last equality follows from the definition of the conditional variance and the law of iterated expectation.

## G.2Proof forLemma˜3

The sigma-field inclusion implies that each coordinateVjV_{j}is measurable with respect toσ​(U)¯P\overline{\sigma(U)}^{P}. By Proposition 2.12 ofFolland (1999), there exists aσ​(U)\sigma(U)-measurable random variableV~j\widetilde{V}_{j}such thatV~j=Vj\widetilde{V}_{j}=V_{j},PP-a.s.
For eachj=1,…,qj=1,\ldots,q, Theorem 4.2.8 ofDudley (2002)yields a measurable functionΓj:𝒰→ℝ\Gamma_{j}:\mathcal{U}\to\mathbb{R}such thatV~j=Γj∘U\widetilde{V}_{j}=\Gamma_{j}\circ U. Hence,Γ=(Γ1,…,Γq)′:𝒰→ℝq\Gamma=(\Gamma_{1},\ldots,\Gamma_{q})^{\prime}:\mathcal{U}\to\mathbb{R}^{q}is measurable and satisfiesV~=Γ∘U\widetilde{V}=\Gamma\circ U.
SinceV=V~V=\widetilde{V},PP-a.s., the result follows.

## G.4Proof forLemma˜5

## Proof.

The assumed Euclidean-norm bounds imply thatsupz∈𝒵|ft​(z)|,supz∈𝒵|ft∗​(z)|≤M,t=1,…,T.\sup_{z\in\mathcal{Z}}|f_{t}(z)|,\sup_{z\in\mathcal{Z}}|f_{t}^{*}(z)|\leq M,\qquad t=1,\ldots,T.

The constantscLc_{L}andcUc_{U}can therefore be derived as in Lemma 9 ofFarrellet al.(2021).

It remains to verify the Lipschitz constant. Foru∈ℝTu\in\mathbb{R}^{T}, definept​(u):=exp⁡(ut)1+∑j=1Texp⁡(uj),t=1,…,T.p_{t}(u):=\frac{\exp(u_{t})}{1+\sum_{j=1}^{T}\exp(u_{j})},\qquad t=1,\ldots,T.

Writep​(u)=(p1​(u),…,pT​(u))′p(u)=(p_{1}(u),\ldots,p_{T}(u))^{\prime}. Fory=1,…,Ty=1,\ldots,T, letey∈ℝTe_{y}\in\mathbb{R}^{T}be theyy-th standard basis vector, and leteT+1=𝟎Te_{T+1}=\mathbf{0}_{T}. Then,∇1ℓ​(u,y)=p​(u)−ey.\nabla_{1}\ell(u,y)=p(u)-e_{y}.

Fory=1,…,Ty=1,\ldots,T,‖∇1ℓ​(u,y)‖22\displaystyle\|\nabla_{1}\ell(u,y)\|_{2}^{2}=(1−py​(u))2+∑j≠ypj​(u)2\displaystyle=(1-p_{y}(u))^{2}+\sum_{j\neq y}p_{j}(u)^{2}≤(1−py​(u))2+(∑j≠ypj​(u))2\displaystyle\leq(1-p_{y}(u))^{2}+\left(\sum_{j\neq y}p_{j}(u)\right)^{2}≤2​(1−py​(u))2\displaystyle\leq 2(1-p_{y}(u))^{2}≤2,\displaystyle\leq 2,

where the first inequality follows frompj​(u)≥0p_{j}(u)\geq 0and the second inequality follows from∑j≠ypj​(u)≤1−py​(u)\sum_{j\neq y}p_{j}(u)\leq 1-p_{y}(u).
We also have‖∇1ℓ​(u,T+1)‖2=‖p​(u)‖2≤1\|\nabla_{1}\ell(u,T+1)\|_{2}=\|p(u)\|_{2}\leq 1. The fundamental theorem of calculus and the Cauchy-Schwarz inequality now give|ℓ​(f​(z),y)−ℓ​(a​(z),y)|\displaystyle|\ell(f(z),y)-\ell(a(z),y)|≤sups∈[0,1]‖∇1ℓ​(a​(z)+s​(f​(z)−a​(z)),y)‖2​‖f​(z)−a​(z)‖2\displaystyle\leq\sup_{s\in[0,1]}\|\nabla_{1}\ell(a(z)+s(f(z)-a(z)),y)\|_{2}\|f(z)-a(z)\|_{2}≤2​‖f​(z)−a​(z)‖2.\displaystyle\leq\sqrt{2}\|f(z)-a(z)\|_{2}.

## G.7Proof forLemma˜8

## Proof.

For every discrete probability measureQQand everyh∈ℋmh\in\mathcal{H}_{m}, definedQ,h​(f,g)=‖f∘h−g∘h‖Q,2.d_{Q,h}(f,g)=\|f\circ h-g\circ h\|_{Q,2}.

For everyh∈ℋmh\in\mathcal{H}_{m}andf∈Bn,h​(δ)f\in B_{n,h}(\delta),n​[(𝕄n−Mn)​(f,h)−(𝕄n−Mn)​(fn,h∗,h)]=𝔾n​(mf,h−mfn,h∗,h).\sqrt{n}\left[\left(\mathbb{M}_{n}-M_{n}\right)(f,h)-\left(\mathbb{M}_{n}-M_{n}\right)\left(f_{n,h}^{*},h\right)\right]=\mathbb{G}_{n}\left(m_{f,h}-m_{f_{n,h}^{*},h}\right).

Hence,supf∈Bn,h​(δ)n​|(𝕄n−Mn)​(f,h)−(𝕄n−Mn)​(fn,h∗,h)|=‖𝔾n‖ℳn,h​(δ).\sup_{f\in B_{n,h}(\delta)}\sqrt{n}\left|\left(\mathbb{M}_{n}-M_{n}\right)(f,h)-\left(\mathbb{M}_{n}-M_{n}\right)\left(f_{n,h}^{*},h\right)\right|=\left\|\mathbb{G}_{n}\right\|_{\mathcal{M}_{n,h}(\delta)}.

Fixh∈ℋmh\in\mathcal{H}_{m}and letQQbe any discrete probability measure.
LetQhQ_{h}denote the image ofQQunderhh.
For everyf,f~∈Bn,h​(δ)f,\widetilde{f}\in B_{n,h}(\delta), the Lipschitz continuity ofℓ\ellimplies that, for every(z,y)(z,y),|mf,h​(z,y)−mf~,h​(z,y)|=|ℓ​(f∘h​(z),y)−ℓ​(f~∘h​(z),y)|≤Cℓ​‖f∘h​(z)−f~∘h​(z)‖2.\left|m_{f,h}(z,y)-m_{\widetilde{f},h}(z,y)\right|=\left|\ell\left(f\circ h(z),y\right)-\ell\left(\widetilde{f}\circ h(z),y\right)\right|\leq C_{\ell}\left\|f\circ h(z)-\widetilde{f}\circ h(z)\right\|_{2}.

Hence,‖(mf,h−mfn,h∗,h)−(mf~,h−mfn,h∗,h)‖Q,2\displaystyle\left\|\left(m_{f,h}-m_{f_{n,h}^{*},h}\right)-\left(m_{\widetilde{f},h}-m_{f_{n,h}^{*},h}\right)\right\|_{Q,2}=‖mf,h−mf~,h‖Q,2\displaystyle=\left\|m_{f,h}-m_{\widetilde{f},h}\right\|_{Q,2}≤Cℓ​‖f∘h−f~∘h‖Q,2\displaystyle\leq C_{\ell}\left\|f\circ h-\widetilde{f}\circ h\right\|_{Q,2}=Cℓ​dQ,h​(f,f~).\displaystyle=C_{\ell}d_{Q,h}(f,\widetilde{f}).(52)

Define the normalized classℳ~n,h​(δ)={ℓdiff2​Cℓ​M:ℓdiff∈ℳn,h​(δ)}.\widetilde{\mathcal{M}}_{n,h}(\delta)=\left\{\frac{\ell_{\mathrm{diff}}}{2C_{\ell}M}:\ell_{\mathrm{diff}}\in\mathcal{M}_{n,h}(\delta)\right\}.

We haveN​(ε/(2​Cℓ​M),ℳ~n,h​(δ),L2​(Q))\displaystyle N\left(\varepsilon/(2C_{\ell}M),\widetilde{\mathcal{M}}_{n,h}(\delta),L_{2}(Q)\right)=N​(ε,ℳn,h​(δ),L2​(Q))\displaystyle=N\left(\varepsilon,\mathcal{M}_{n,h}(\delta),L_{2}(Q)\right)≤N​(ε/Cℓ,Bn,h​(δ),dQ,h)\displaystyle\leq N\left(\varepsilon/C_{\ell},B_{n,h}(\delta),d_{Q,h}\right)≤N​(ε/Cℓ,ℱn,dQ,h),\displaystyle\leq N\left(\varepsilon/C_{\ell},\mathcal{F}_{n},d_{Q,h}\right),(53)

where the first inequality follows from˜52and the second inequality follows from the fact thatBn,h​(δ)⊆ℱnB_{n,h}(\delta)\subseteq\mathcal{F}_{n}.
By the definition ofQhQ_{h},dQ,h​(f,g)=‖f∘h−g∘h‖Q,2=‖f−g‖Qh,2.d_{Q,h}(f,g)=\|f\circ h-g\circ h\|_{Q,2}=\|f-g\|_{Q_{h},2}.

## G.8Proof forLemma˜9

## Proof.

Letℱ0⊂ℱ\mathcal{F}^{0}\subset\mathcal{F}be a countable subset such that for everyf∈ℱf\in\mathcal{F}, there exists a sequence{fj}j=1∞\{f_{j}\}_{j=1}^{\infty}inℱ0\mathcal{F}^{0}such that‖fj​(x)−f​(x)‖2→0\|f_{j}(x)-f(x)\|_{2}\rightarrow 0for everyxx.
Pick an arbitraryf∈B​(δ)f\in B(\delta). We haveP​‖f−f∗‖22<δ2P\|f-f^{*}\|_{2}^{2}<\delta^{2}.
The continuity of the squared Euclidean norm implies that‖fj​(x)−f∗​(x)‖22→‖f​(x)−f∗​(x)‖22\|f_{j}(x)-f^{*}(x)\|_{2}^{2}\rightarrow\|f(x)-f^{*}(x)\|_{2}^{2}for everyxx. Moreover, since‖fj​(x)‖2≤F​(x)\|f_{j}(x)\|_{2}\leq F(x)and‖f∗​(x)‖2≤F​(x)\|f^{*}(x)\|_{2}\leq F(x), we have‖fj​(x)−f∗​(x)‖22≤4​F​(x)2\|f_{j}(x)-f^{*}(x)\|_{2}^{2}\leq 4F(x)^{2}. SinceP​F2<∞PF^{2}<\infty, applying the dominated convergence theorem to the scalar functions‖fj​(x)−f∗​(x)‖22\|f_{j}(x)-f^{*}(x)\|_{2}^{2}yieldsP​‖fj−f∗‖22→P​‖f−f∗‖22<δ2.P\|f_{j}-f^{*}\|_{2}^{2}\rightarrow P\|f-f^{*}\|_{2}^{2}<\delta^{2}.

Therefore,fj∈B​(δ)f_{j}\in B(\delta)eventually. Hence, everyf∈B​(δ)f\in B(\delta)is the pointwise limit of a sequence in the countable setℱ0∩B​(δ)\mathcal{F}^{0}\cap B(\delta), which proves thatB​(δ)B(\delta)is pointwise measurable.
∎

## G.13Proof forLemma˜14

## Proof.

IfLϕ=0L_{\phi}=0, the Lipschitz condition andϕi​(0)=0\phi_{i}(0)=0imply thatϕi​(u)=0\phi_{i}(u)=0for everyu∈ℝu\in\mathbb{R}and everyii, so the conclusion follows immediately.
Suppose henceforth thatLϕ>0L_{\phi}>0.
By the contraction inequality (Ledoux and Talagrand,1991, Theorem 4.12), for any contractionϕ0,i:ℝ→ℝ\phi_{0,i}:\mathbb{R}\rightarrow\mathbb{R}, we have𝔼​[supu∈𝒰|∑i=1nξi​ϕ0,i​(ui)|]≤2⋅𝔼​[supu∈𝒰|∑i=1nξi​ui|].\mathbb{E}\left[\sup_{u\in\mathcal{U}}\left|\sum_{i=1}^{n}\xi_{i}\phi_{0,i}(u_{i})\right|\right]\leq 2\cdot\mathbb{E}\left[\sup_{u\in\mathcal{U}}\left|\sum_{i=1}^{n}\xi_{i}u_{i}\right|\right].

For a Lipschitz functionϕi:ℝ→ℝ\phi_{i}:\mathbb{R}\rightarrow\mathbb{R}with Lipschitz constantLϕL_{\phi}, we can defineϕ0,i​(x)=ϕi​(x)/Lϕ\phi_{0,i}(x)=\phi_{i}(x)/L_{\phi}, which is a contraction. Then,𝔼​[supu∈𝒰|∑i=1nξi​ϕi​(ui)|]\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\sup_{u\in\mathcal{U}}\left|\sum_{i=1}^{n}\xi_{i}\phi_{i}(u_{i})\right|\right]=Lϕ⋅𝔼​[supu∈𝒰|∑i=1nξi​ϕ0,i​(ui)|]\displaystyle=L_{\phi}\cdot\mathbb{E}\left[\sup_{u\in\mathcal{U}}\left|\sum_{i=1}^{n}\xi_{i}\phi_{0,i}(u_{i})\right|\right]≤2​Lϕ⋅𝔼​[supu∈𝒰|∑i=1nξi​ui|].\displaystyle\leq 2L_{\phi}\cdot\mathbb{E}\left[\sup_{u\in\mathcal{U}}\left|\sum_{i=1}^{n}\xi_{i}u_{i}\right|\right].

This completes the proof.

## Appendix HMultimodal Transfer Learning

In applications, the economist may have access to several pre-trained models, each trained on a different modality. For example, a computer scientist may train a CNN on images and a transformer on text, after which the economist uses both learned embeddings as inputs to a target model. This section extends our transfer-learning theory to this multimodal setting.

Letr=1,…,Rr=1,\ldots,Rindex the modalities, whereRRis fixed as the sample sizes diverge. For eachrr, writemr=mr,nm_{r}=m_{r,n}and suppose thatmr,n→∞m_{r,n}\to\inftyasn→∞n\to\infty. The target observations satisfy(Yi,Z0,i,Z1,i,…,ZR,i)∼i.i.d.Pta,i=1,…,n,(Y_{i},Z_{0,i},Z_{1,i},\ldots,Z_{R,i})\sim_{i.i.d.}P_{\operatorname{ta}},\qquad i=1,\ldots,n,

whereZ0,iZ_{0,i}denotes the structured covariates andZr,i∈𝒵rZ_{r,i}\in\mathcal{Z}_{r}denotes the unstructured input for modalityrr. WriteZi=(Z0,i,Z1,i,…,ZR,i)Z_{i}=(Z_{0,i},Z_{1,i},\ldots,Z_{R,i}). The structured covariates may be low- or high-dimensional. For each modalityrr, the computer scientist observes a source sample(Sr,j,Zr,j)∼i.i.d.Pso,r,j=1,…,mr,(S_{r,j},Z_{r,j})\sim_{i.i.d.}P_{\operatorname{so},r},\qquad j=1,\ldots,m_{r},

where the modality-specific source samples and the target sample are mutually independent.
LetPPdenote the joint distribution of these samples, and let𝔼so,r\mathbb{E}_{\operatorname{so},r}denote expectation underPso,rP_{\operatorname{so},r}.

Letℓso,r\ell_{\operatorname{so},r}be the source loss for modalityrr, and let𝒢r,mr\mathcal{G}_{r,m_{r}}andℋr,mr\mathcal{H}_{r,m_{r}}be the corresponding classes of source heads and embedding functions. For each modality, define the set of population source-risk minimizers byℛr,mr=arg​mingr∈𝒢r,mr,hr∈ℋr,mr⁡𝔼so,r​[ℓso,r​(gr∘hr​(Zr),Sr)].\mathcal{R}_{r,m_{r}}=\operatorname*{arg\,min}_{g_{r}\in\mathcal{G}_{r,m_{r}},h_{r}\in\mathcal{H}_{r,m_{r}}}\mathbb{E}_{\operatorname{so},r}[\ell_{\operatorname{so},r}(g_{r}\circ h_{r}(Z_{r}),S_{r})].(67)

For each modalityrr, let(gwidecheckr,hwidecheckr)∈arg​mingr∈𝒢r,mr,hr∈ℋr,mr⁡1mr​∑j=1mrℓso,r​(gr∘hr​(Zr,j),Sr,j).(\widecheck{g}_{r},\widecheck{h}_{r})\in\operatorname*{arg\,min}_{g_{r}\in\mathcal{G}_{r,m_{r}},h_{r}\in\mathcal{H}_{r,m_{r}}}\frac{1}{m_{r}}\sum_{j=1}^{m_{r}}\ell_{\operatorname{so},r}(g_{r}\circ h_{r}(Z_{r,j}),S_{r,j}).(68)

Thus,hwidecheckr\widecheck{h}_{r}is the pre-trained embedding estimated from the modality-rrsource sample.

Define𝐦=(m1,…,mR)\mathbf{m}=(m_{1},\ldots,m_{R})andℋ𝐦=∏r=1Rℋr,mr\mathcal{H}_{\mathbf{m}}=\prod_{r=1}^{R}\mathcal{H}_{r,m_{r}}. For every multimodal embeddingh=(h1,…,hR)∈ℋ𝐦h=(h_{1},\ldots,h_{R})\in\mathcal{H}_{\mathbf{m}}, define(f∘h)​(Z):=f​(Z0,h1​(Z1),…,hR​(ZR)).(f\circ h)(Z):=f(Z_{0},h_{1}(Z_{1}),\ldots,h_{R}(Z_{R})).

After introducing the multimodal representation, we define the population target-risk minimizer correspondence. For everyh∈ℋ𝐦h\in\mathcal{H}_{\mathbf{m}}, letℱnsub​(h)=arg​minf∈ℱnsub⁡𝔼ta​[ℓta​((f∘h)​(Z),Y)].\mathcal{F}_{n}^{\operatorname{sub}}(h)=\operatorname*{arg\,min}_{f\in\mathcal{F}_{n}^{\operatorname{sub}}}\mathbb{E}_{\operatorname{ta}}[\ell_{\operatorname{ta}}((f\circ h)(Z),Y)].(69)

Given the estimated multimodal embeddinghwidecheck=(hwidecheck1,…,hwidecheckR)\widecheck{h}=(\widecheck{h}_{1},\ldots,\widecheck{h}_{R}), the economist estimatesf^∈arg​minf∈ℱn⁡1n​∑i=1nℓta​((f∘hwidecheck)​(Zi),Yi).\widehat{f}\in\operatorname*{arg\,min}_{f\in\mathcal{F}_{n}}\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\ell_{\operatorname{ta}}((f\circ\widecheck{h})(Z_{i}),Y_{i}).

## Definition 6(Modality-Wise Diversity and Transferability).

Fix population minimizers(gr,mr,hr,mr)∈ℛr,mr(g_{r,m_{r}},h_{r,m_{r}})\in\mathcal{R}_{r,m_{r}}forr=1,…,Rr=1,\ldots,Randfn∈ℱnsub​(h𝐦)f_{n}\in\mathcal{F}_{n}^{\operatorname{sub}}(h_{\mathbf{m}}), whereh𝐦=(h1,m1,…,hR,mR)h_{\mathbf{m}}=(h_{1,m_{1}},\ldots,h_{R,m_{R}}).
For each modalityrrandρ≥0\rho\geq 0, define the source-side approximate identified set byℋso,r,mr​(ρ):={hr∈ℋr,mr:mingr∈𝒢r,mr⁡‖gr∘hr−gr,mr∘hr,mr‖Pso,r,2≤ρ}.\mathcal{H}_{\operatorname{so},r,m_{r}}(\rho):=\left\{h_{r}\in\mathcal{H}_{r,m_{r}}:\min_{g_{r}\in\mathcal{G}_{r,m_{r}}}\|g_{r}\circ h_{r}-g_{r,m_{r}}\circ h_{r,m_{r}}\|_{P_{\operatorname{so},r},2}\leq\rho\right\}.

Forh−r=(h1,…,hr−1,hr+1,…,hR)∈∏s≠rℋs,msh_{-r}=(h_{1},\ldots,h_{r-1},h_{r+1},\ldots,h_{R})\in\prod_{s\neq r}\mathcal{H}_{s,m_{s}}, let(hr,h−r)(h_{r},h_{-r})denote the multimodal embedding whoserr-th component ishrh_{r}. A jointly selected family of population target models is a collection{fn,h:h∈ℋ𝐦}\{f_{n,h}:h\in\mathcal{H}_{\mathbf{m}}\}satisfyingfn,h∈ℱnsub​(h),h∈ℋ𝐦,f_{n,h}\in\mathcal{F}_{n}^{\operatorname{sub}}(h),\qquad h\in\mathcal{H}_{\mathbf{m}},(70)

andfn,h𝐦=fnf_{n,h_{\mathbf{m}}}=f_{n}. Given such a family, defineDr,n​(hr∣h−r):=‖fn,(hr,h−r)∘(hr,h−r)−fn,(hr,mr,h−r)∘(hr,mr,h−r)‖Pta,2D_{r,n}(h_{r}\mid h_{-r}):=\|f_{n,(h_{r},h_{-r})}\circ(h_{r},h_{-r})-f_{n,(h_{r,m_{r}},h_{-r})}\circ(h_{r,m_{r}},h_{-r})\|_{P_{\operatorname{ta}},2}

and the target-side neighborhood induced by this familyℋta,r,n​(ρ∣h−r):={hr∈ℋr,mr:Dr,n​(hr∣h−r)≤ρ}.\mathcal{H}_{\operatorname{ta},r,n}(\rho\mid h_{-r}):=\left\{h_{r}\in\mathcal{H}_{r,m_{r}}:D_{r,n}(h_{r}\mid h_{-r})\leq\rho\right\}.

Relative to this family, we say that the modality-rrsource taskgr,mrg_{r,m_{r}}is(ρso,r,mr,ρta,r,n)(\rho_{\operatorname{so},r,m_{r}},\rho_{\operatorname{ta},r,n})-diverse overfnf_{n}with respect tohr,mrh_{r,m_{r}}ifℋso,r,mr​(ρso,r,mr)⊆ℋta,r,n​(ρta,r,n∣h−r)\mathcal{H}_{\operatorname{so},r,m_{r}}(\rho_{\operatorname{so},r,m_{r}})\subseteq\mathcal{H}_{\operatorname{ta},r,n}(\rho_{\operatorname{ta},r,n}\mid h_{-r})

holds for everyh−r∈∏s≠rℋs,msh_{-r}\in\prod_{s\neq r}\mathcal{H}_{s,m_{s}}. Letνn,r>0\nu_{n,r}>0for everynnandr=1,…,Rr=1,\ldots,R. Given a sequence of jointly selected families, we say that, relative to this sequence, the modality-rrsource taskgr,mrg_{r,m_{r}}isνn,r\nu_{n,r}-transferable tofnf_{n}with respect tohr,mrh_{r,m_{r}}at rateδso,r,mr\delta_{\operatorname{so},r,m_{r}}if, for every sequenceρso,r,mr=O​(δso,r,mr)\rho_{\operatorname{so},r,m_{r}}=O(\delta_{\operatorname{so},r,m_{r}}), the(ρso,r,mr,ρso,r,mr/νn,r)(\rho_{\operatorname{so},r,m_{r}},\rho_{\operatorname{so},r,m_{r}}/\nu_{n,r})-diversity condition holds for all sufficiently largenn.
We say that these modality-wise diversity conditions hold jointly if there exists a single jointly selected family relative to which they hold simultaneously for everyr=1,…,Rr=1,\ldots,Rand everyh−r∈∏s≠rℋs,msh_{-r}\in\prod_{s\neq r}\mathcal{H}_{s,m_{s}}. We say that modality-wise transferability holds jointly if there exists a single sequence of jointly selected families relative to which the preceding transferability condition holds simultaneously for everyr=1,…,Rr=1,\ldots,R.

This definition is the modality-wise analogue ofDefinition˜2. The joint condition requires a common selected population target model family at eachnnsuch that inclusion in each modality-specific source-side approximate identified set implies inclusion in the corresponding target-side neighborhood for every configuration of the other embeddings. This common selection permits the modalities to be replaced sequentially by their estimated embeddings.

## Theorem 9(Convergence Rate for Multimodal Transfer Learning).

Fix(gr,mr,hr,mr)∈ℛr,mr(g_{r,m_{r}},h_{r,m_{r}})\in\mathcal{R}_{r,m_{r}}forr=1,…,Rr=1,\ldots,Randfn∈ℱnsub​(h𝐦)f_{n}\in\mathcal{F}_{n}^{\operatorname{sub}}(h_{\mathbf{m}}). Define∥fn∘h𝐦−ata∗∥Pta,2=:ϵta,n.\|f_{n}\circ h_{\mathbf{m}}-a_{\operatorname{ta}}^{*}\|_{P_{\operatorname{ta}},2}=:\epsilon_{\operatorname{ta},n}.

Givenh𝑤𝑖𝑑𝑒𝑐ℎ𝑒𝑐𝑘\widecheck{h}, define theL2​(Pta)L^{2}(P_{\operatorname{ta}})-best approximation tofn∘h𝐦f_{n}\circ h_{\mathbf{m}}byfn,hwidecheck∙∈arg​minf∈ℱnsub⁡‖f∘hwidecheck−fn∘h𝐦‖Pta,2.f_{n,\widecheck{h}}^{\bullet}\in\operatorname*{arg\,min}_{f\in\mathcal{F}_{n}^{\operatorname{sub}}}\|f\circ\widecheck{h}-f_{n}\circ h_{\mathbf{m}}\|_{P_{\operatorname{ta}},2}.

For each modalityr=1,…,Rr=1,\ldots,R, suppose thatνn,r>0\nu_{n,r}>0for everynnand that‖gwidecheckr∘hwidecheckr−gr,mr∘hr,mr‖Pso,r,2=OPso,r​(δso,r,mr).\|\widecheck{g}_{r}\circ\widecheck{h}_{r}-g_{r,m_{r}}\circ h_{r,m_{r}}\|_{P_{\operatorname{so},r},2}=O_{P_{\operatorname{so},r}}(\delta_{\operatorname{so},r,m_{r}}).

Suppose also that modality-wise transferability holds jointly in the sense ofDefinition˜6; that is, there exists a common sequence of jointly selected population target model families relative to whichgr,mrg_{r,m_{r}}isνn,r\nu_{n,r}-transferable tofnf_{n}with respect tohr,mrh_{r,m_{r}}at rateδso,r,mr\delta_{\operatorname{so},r,m_{r}}for everyr=1,…,Rr=1,\ldots,R. Suppose further that‖f^∘hwidecheck−fn,hwidecheck∙∘hwidecheck‖Pta,2=OP​(rta,n).\|\widehat{f}\circ\widecheck{h}-f_{n,\widecheck{h}}^{\bullet}\circ\widecheck{h}\|_{P_{\operatorname{ta}},2}=O_{P}(r_{\operatorname{ta},n}).

Then,‖f^∘hwidecheck−ata∗‖Pta,2=OP​(rta,n+∑r=1Rδso,r,mrνn,r+ϵta,n).\|\widehat{f}\circ\widecheck{h}-a_{\operatorname{ta}}^{*}\|_{P_{\operatorname{ta}},2}=O_{P}\left(r_{\operatorname{ta},n}+\sum_{r=1}^{R}\frac{\delta_{\operatorname{so},r,m_{r}}}{\nu_{n,r}}+\epsilon_{\operatorname{ta},n}\right).

## Proof.

Fix a common sequence of jointly selected population target model families witnessing simultaneous transferability for every modality.
Forr=0,…,Rr=0,\ldots,R, define the intermediate multimodal embeddingsh~(r):=(hwidecheck1,…,hwidecheckr,hr+1,mr+1,…,hR,mR).\widetilde{h}^{(r)}:=(\widecheck{h}_{1},\ldots,\widecheck{h}_{r},h_{r+1,m_{r+1}},\ldots,h_{R,m_{R}}).

Thus,h~(0)=h𝐦\widetilde{h}^{(0)}=h_{\mathbf{m}}andh~(R)=hwidecheck\widetilde{h}^{(R)}=\widecheck{h}. The triangle inequality yields‖f^∘hwidecheck−ata∗‖Pta,2\displaystyle\|\widehat{f}\circ\widecheck{h}-a_{\operatorname{ta}}^{*}\|_{P_{\operatorname{ta}},2}≤‖f^∘hwidecheck−fn,hwidecheck∙∘hwidecheck‖Pta,2\displaystyle\leq\|\widehat{f}\circ\widecheck{h}-f_{n,\widecheck{h}}^{\bullet}\circ\widecheck{h}\|_{P_{\operatorname{ta}},2}+‖fn,hwidecheck∙∘hwidecheck−fn∘h𝐦‖Pta,2\displaystyle\quad+\|f_{n,\widecheck{h}}^{\bullet}\circ\widecheck{h}-f_{n}\circ h_{\mathbf{m}}\|_{P_{\operatorname{ta}},2}+‖fn∘h𝐦−ata∗‖Pta,2.\displaystyle\quad+\|f_{n}\circ h_{\mathbf{m}}-a_{\operatorname{ta}}^{*}\|_{P_{\operatorname{ta}},2}.

The first term on the right-hand side isOP​(rta,n)O_{P}(r_{\operatorname{ta},n}), and the last term equalsϵta,n\epsilon_{\operatorname{ta},n}. By the definition offn,hwidecheck∙f_{n,\widecheck{h}}^{\bullet}, the fact thatfn,hwidecheck∈ℱnsub​(hwidecheck)⊆ℱnsubf_{n,\widecheck{h}}\in\mathcal{F}_{n}^{\operatorname{sub}}(\widecheck{h})\subseteq\mathcal{F}_{n}^{\operatorname{sub}}, and the triangle inequality,‖fn,hwidecheck∙∘hwidecheck−fn∘h𝐦‖Pta,2\displaystyle\|f_{n,\widecheck{h}}^{\bullet}\circ\widecheck{h}-f_{n}\circ h_{\mathbf{m}}\|_{P_{\operatorname{ta}},2}≤‖fn,hwidecheck∘hwidecheck−fn∘h𝐦‖Pta,2\displaystyle\leq\|f_{n,\widecheck{h}}\circ\widecheck{h}-f_{n}\circ h_{\mathbf{m}}\|_{P_{\operatorname{ta}},2}≤∑r=1R‖fn,h~(r)∘h~(r)−fn,h~(r−1)∘h~(r−1)‖Pta,2,\displaystyle\leq\sum_{r=1}^{R}\|f_{n,\widetilde{h}^{(r)}}\circ\widetilde{h}^{(r)}-f_{n,\widetilde{h}^{(r-1)}}\circ\widetilde{h}^{(r-1)}\|_{P_{\operatorname{ta}},2},

where the second inequality usesfn,h~(0)=fnf_{n,\widetilde{h}^{(0)}}=f_{n}andfn,h~(R)=fn,hwidecheckf_{n,\widetilde{h}^{(R)}}=f_{n,\widecheck{h}}.

For each modalityrr,gwidecheckr∈𝒢r,mr\widecheck{g}_{r}\in\mathcal{G}_{r,m_{r}}and the source-rate condition implymingr∈𝒢r,mr⁡‖gr∘hwidecheckr−gr,mr∘hr,mr‖Pso,r,2\displaystyle\min_{g_{r}\in\mathcal{G}_{r,m_{r}}}\|g_{r}\circ\widecheck{h}_{r}-g_{r,m_{r}}\circ h_{r,m_{r}}\|_{P_{\operatorname{so},r},2}≤‖gwidecheckr∘hwidecheckr−gr,mr∘hr,mr‖Pso,r,2\displaystyle\leq\|\widecheck{g}_{r}\circ\widecheck{h}_{r}-g_{r,m_{r}}\circ h_{r,m_{r}}\|_{P_{\operatorname{so},r},2}=OPso,r​(δso,r,mr).\displaystyle=O_{P_{\operatorname{so},r}}(\delta_{\operatorname{so},r,m_{r}}).

Each source-rate event depends only on the modality-rrsource sample, so the same probability bound holds under the joint lawPP. Fixε>0\varepsilon>0. For eachrr, the source-rate condition gives a constantMε,r<∞M_{\varepsilon,r}<\inftysuch that for all sufficiently largennand correspondingmrm_{r},P​(hwidecheckr∉ℋso,r,mr​(Mε,r​δso,r,mr))≤εR.P\left(\widecheck{h}_{r}\notin\mathcal{H}_{\operatorname{so},r,m_{r}}(M_{\varepsilon,r}\delta_{\operatorname{so},r,m_{r}})\right)\leq\frac{\varepsilon}{R}.

LetMε=max1≤r≤R⁡Mε,rM_{\varepsilon}=\max_{1\leq r\leq R}M_{\varepsilon,r}. SinceRRis fixed, the union bound implies that the eventℰn:=⋂r=1R{hwidecheckr∈ℋso,r,mr​(Mε​δso,r,mr)}\mathcal{E}_{n}:=\bigcap_{r=1}^{R}\left\{\widecheck{h}_{r}\in\mathcal{H}_{\operatorname{so},r,m_{r}}(M_{\varepsilon}\delta_{\operatorname{so},r,m_{r}})\right\}

satisfiesP​(ℰn)≥1−∑r=1RεR=1−εP(\mathcal{E}_{n})\geq 1-\sum_{r=1}^{R}\frac{\varepsilon}{R}=1-\varepsilon

for all sufficiently largennand correspondingmrm_{r}.

The embeddingsh~(r)\widetilde{h}^{(r)}andh~(r−1)\widetilde{h}^{(r-1)}differ only in theirrr-th components. Therefore, onℰn\mathcal{E}_{n}, modality-wise transferability applies with the remaining components fixed at(hwidecheck1,…,hwidecheckr−1,hr+1,mr+1,…,hR,mR)(\widecheck{h}_{1},\ldots,\widecheck{h}_{r-1},h_{r+1,m_{r+1}},\ldots,h_{R,m_{R}})and yieldsDr,n​(hwidecheckr∣(hwidecheck1,…,hwidecheckr−1,hr+1,mr+1,…,hR,mR))\displaystyle D_{r,n}\left(\widecheck{h}_{r}\mid\left(\widecheck{h}_{1},\ldots,\widecheck{h}_{r-1},h_{r+1,m_{r+1}},\ldots,h_{R,m_{R}}\right)\right)=‖fn,h~(r)∘h~(r)−fn,h~(r−1)∘h~(r−1)‖Pta,2\displaystyle=\|f_{n,\widetilde{h}^{(r)}}\circ\widetilde{h}^{(r)}-f_{n,\widetilde{h}^{(r-1)}}\circ\widetilde{h}^{(r-1)}\|_{P_{\operatorname{ta}},2}≤Mε​δso,r,mrνn,r.\displaystyle\leq\frac{M_{\varepsilon}\delta_{\operatorname{so},r,m_{r}}}{\nu_{n,r}}.

It follows that∑r=1R‖fn,h~(r)∘h~(r)−fn,h~(r−1)∘h~(r−1)‖Pta,2=OP​(∑r=1Rδso,r,mrνn,r).\sum_{r=1}^{R}\|f_{n,\widetilde{h}^{(r)}}\circ\widetilde{h}^{(r)}-f_{n,\widetilde{h}^{(r-1)}}\circ\widetilde{h}^{(r-1)}\|_{P_{\operatorname{ta}},2}=O_{P}\left(\sum_{r=1}^{R}\frac{\delta_{\operatorname{so},r,m_{r}}}{\nu_{n,r}}\right).

Combining these bounds proves the result.
∎

The population target modelsfn,hf_{n,h}used in modality-wise transferability are target-risk minimizers, whereasfn,hwidecheck∙f_{n,\widecheck{h}}^{\bullet}in the target-stage condition is theL2​(Pta)L^{2}(P_{\operatorname{ta}})-best approximation tofn∘h𝐦f_{n}\circ h_{\mathbf{m}}. The best-approximation property links these objects in the proof.

The theorem shows that, under modality-wise transferability, the representation error accumulates additively across modalities. In the image-text special case withR=2R=2, the bound becomes‖f^∘hwidecheck−ata∗‖Pta,2\displaystyle\|\widehat{f}\circ\widecheck{h}-a_{\operatorname{ta}}^{*}\|_{P_{\operatorname{ta}},2}=OP​(rta,n+δso,image,mimageνn,image+δso,text,mtextνn,text+ϵta,n).\displaystyle=O_{P}\left(r_{\operatorname{ta},n}+\frac{\delta_{\operatorname{so},\mathrm{image},m_{\mathrm{image}}}}{\nu_{n,\mathrm{image}}}+\frac{\delta_{\operatorname{so},\mathrm{text},m_{\mathrm{text}}}}{\nu_{n,\mathrm{text}}}+\epsilon_{\operatorname{ta},n}\right).

Thus, the convergence rate of the target estimator depends on the sum of the source rates for each modality, adjusted by the corresponding transferability coefficients.

## Appendix ISimulation

This section reports the Monte Carlo design for the partially linear model inExample˜1.
The goal is to illustrate how the downstream DML estimator behaves when the target nuisance functions are aligned with the source-task representation and when they contain a component that is not identified from the source task.

## I.1Design

In each replication, the target sample satisfiesYi\displaystyle Y_{i}=Di​θ0+h∗​(Zita)′​β0+Ui,\displaystyle=D_{i}\theta_{0}+h^{*}(Z_{i}^{\operatorname{ta}})^{\prime}\beta_{0}+U_{i},Di\displaystyle D_{i}=h∗​(Zita)′​δ0+Vi,\displaystyle=h^{*}(Z_{i}^{\operatorname{ta}})^{\prime}\delta_{0}+V_{i},

with layer dimensions(d0,d1,d2,d3,d4,d5)=(50,64,64,64,64,10)(d_{0},d_{1},d_{2},d_{3},d_{4},d_{5})=(50,64,64,64,64,10),Wℓ∈ℝdℓ−1×dℓW_{\ell}\in\mathbb{R}^{d_{\ell-1}\times d_{\ell}}, andbℓ∈ℝdℓb_{\ell}\in\mathbb{R}^{d_{\ell}}.

whereT=100T=100andB=U​AB=UAwithU∈ℝ10×5U\in\mathbb{R}^{10\times 5}having orthonormal columns andA∈ℝ5×100A\in\mathbb{R}^{5\times 100}Gaussian.

## I.2Estimation

For each replication, the source stage estimates a five-layer ReLU representation with the same architecture as the trueh∗h^{*}using the mean squared error on the source sample.
The reported run usesR=2000R=2000replications, target sample sizen=500n=500, source sample sizem=2000m=2000,T=100T=100source outputs,σso=σU=1\sigma_{\operatorname{so}}=\sigma_{U}=1,σV=0.5\sigma_{V}=0.5, five-fold cross-fitting,2020source-training epochs, batch size128128, and learning rate10−310^{-3}.

Given the learned embeddinghwidecheck\widecheck{h}, each cross-fitting split estimates linear projections ofYiY_{i},DiD_{i}, andYi−θ0​DiY_{i}-\theta_{0}D_{i}onhwidecheck​(Zi)\widecheck{h}(Z_{i}):ℓ^(−k)​(z)\displaystyle\widehat{\ell}^{(-k)}(z)=a^ℓ(−k)+b^ℓ(−k)⁣′​hwidecheck​(z),\displaystyle=\widehat{a}_{\ell}^{(-k)}+\widehat{b}_{\ell}^{(-k)\prime}\widecheck{h}(z),α^(−k)​(z)\displaystyle\widehat{\alpha}^{(-k)}(z)=a^α(−k)+b^α(−k)⁣′​hwidecheck​(z),\displaystyle=\widehat{a}_{\alpha}^{(-k)}+\widehat{b}_{\alpha}^{(-k)\prime}\widecheck{h}(z),γ^(−k)​(z)\displaystyle\widehat{\gamma}^{(-k)}(z)=a^γ(−k)+b^γ(−k)⁣′​hwidecheck​(z).\displaystyle=\widehat{a}_{\gamma}^{(-k)}+\widehat{b}_{\gamma}^{(-k)\prime}\widecheck{h}(z).

The partially linear DML estimator is thenY~i\displaystyle\widetilde{Y}_{i}=Yi−ℓ^(−k​(i))​(Zi),\displaystyle=Y_{i}-\widehat{\ell}^{(-k(i))}(Z_{i}),D~i\displaystyle\widetilde{D}_{i}=Di−α^(−k​(i))​(Zi),\displaystyle=D_{i}-\widehat{\alpha}^{(-k(i))}(Z_{i}),θ^\displaystyle\widehat{\theta}=∑i=1nD~i​Y~i∑i=1nD~i2.\displaystyle=\frac{\sum_{i=1}^{n}\widetilde{D}_{i}\widetilde{Y}_{i}}{\sum_{i=1}^{n}\widetilde{D}_{i}^{2}}.

The estimatorγ^(−k)\widehat{\gamma}^{(-k)}is not used in the final ratio; it is recorded only to measure the prediction error for the nuisance functionγ0\gamma_{0}.

## I.3Monte Carlo Results

Table˜1shows that the in-span design is nearly centered at the truth, while the out-of-span design produces a sizable upward shift in the distribution ofθ^\widehat{\theta}.
The mean ofθ^\widehat{\theta}increases from1.0051.005in the in-span design to1.1971.197in the out-of-span design; thus, the difference in Monte Carlo means is approximately0.1910.191.
This shift is accompanied by much larger nuisance prediction errors in the out-of-span design, as shown inTable˜2.
In particular, the mean squared prediction error forγ0\gamma_{0}increases from0.1100.110to10.57910.579, and the corresponding error forα0\alpha_{0}increases from0.0440.044to2.6772.677.
These patterns are consistent with the theory. When the target nuisance functions contain a component outside the source-identified span, transfer learning does not recover that direction accurately enough for the downstream DML step.CaseMeanSDMedianIn-span1.0050.2121.004Out-of-span1.1970.2281.190Table 1:Empirical distribution ofθ^\widehat{\theta}across 2000 replications.CaseMean PE forγ0\gamma_{0}SD PE forγ0\gamma_{0}Mean PE forα0\alpha_{0}SD PE forα0\alpha_{0}In-span0.1100.8870.0440.372Out-of-span10.579464.7832.677115.744Table 2:Cross-fitted nuisance prediction errors from the saved Monte Carlo run.
